# Proposed OEIS comments: A279130 to A282555

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A279130

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279130>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [633](../papers/00501-01000/00633-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 16*a(n-1) -108*a(n-2) +401*a(n-3) -893*a(n-4) +1179*a(n-5) -600*a(n-6) -1173*a(n-7) +3468*a(n-8) -5018*a(n-9) +6446*a(n-10) -8766*a(n-11) +8587*a(n-12) -1891*a(n-13) -12870*a(n-14) +29298*a(n-15) -31377*a(n-16) +26556*a(n-17) -29210*a(n-18) +21407*a(n-19) +1380*a(n-20) -39341*a(n-21) +76349*a(n-22) -59868*a(n-23) +31048*a(n-24) -37732*a(n-25) +23625*a(n-26) +13127*a(n-27) -35339*a(n-28) +40641*a(n-29) -26406*a(n-30) +5772*a(n-31) +6474*a(n-32) -9682*a(n-33) +6694*a(n-34) -2802*a(n-35) +369*a(n-36) +540*a(n-37) -513*a(n-38) +299*a(n-39) -116*a(n-40) +36*a(n-41) -7*a(n-42) +a(n-43) for n>50

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 771 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 50. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A279498

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279498>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4417](../papers/04001-04500/04417-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Dec 14 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 17 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A279872

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279872>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4577](../papers/04501-05000/04577-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Chai Wah Wu_, Aug 02 2021: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A280367

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A280367>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4517](../papers/04501-05000/04517-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 01 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A280410

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A280410>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4578](../papers/04501-05000/04578-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 03 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281146

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281146>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4521](../papers/04501-05000/04521-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 22 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281214

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281214>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4446](../papers/04001-04500/04446-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 18 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 12 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281473

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281473>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3272](../papers/03001-03500/03272-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-2) + a(n-3) - a(n-4) + a(n-5) -a (n-6) for n>11.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(16 + 232*x - 379*x^2 + 189*x^3 - 251*x^4 + 124*x^5 - 288*x^6 + 132*x^7 - 24*x^8 + x^9 + 2*x^10) / ((1 - x)*(1 - x - x^3 - x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 19 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 561 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A282002

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282002>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4550](../papers/04501-05000/04550-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 05 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 7 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3555](../papers/03501-04000/03555-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A282088

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282088>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4553](../papers/04501-05000/04553-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 27 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 7 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A282125

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282125>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [1198](../papers/01001-01500/01198-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 14*a(n-1) - 57*a(n-2) + 88*a(n-3) - 256*a(n-4) + 240*a(n-5) - 320*a(n-6) + 256*a(n-7) - 64*a(n-8).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x^3*(3 - 2*x + 4*x^2)*(5 + 2*x - 12*x^2) / (1 - 7*x + 4*x^2 - 16*x^3 + 8*x^4)^2. - _Colin Barker_, Feb 20 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 196 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A282313

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282313>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11579](../papers/11501-12000/11579-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 57 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 87 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 57, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical g.f.: x^3 / (1 - 3*x - 3*x^2 - 3*x^3)^2. - _Colin Barker_, Feb 20 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 60 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A282411

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282411>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4522](../papers/04501-05000/04522-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 15 2017: (Start)
> Equivalent conjecture: a(n) = A279118(n) for n>=2. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 21 2025

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A282482

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282482>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4421](../papers/04001-04500/04421-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 18 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 17 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A282526

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282526>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11443](../papers/11001-11500/11443-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 106 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
